> AGENT

**Findings**

- Critical: template management is capability-defined but server handlers only require authentication. `templates.createSimpleTemplate`, `templates.findOne`, `templates.groupedByCategory`, and `templates.updateSimpleTemplate` do not check `templates:create`, `templates:view`, or `templates:editAll`, even though those permissions exist. This means hidden nav is the only barrier for much of the template UI, and direct RPC calls can list, view, create, and update tenant templates. See [templates.handlers.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/server/effect/rpc/handlers/templates.handlers.ts:114) and [permissions.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/shared/permissions/permissions.ts:32).

- Critical: finance transaction history is exposed to any authenticated tenant user. The `/finance` route is only guarded by auth, the nav hides it behind `finance:*`, but `finance.transactions.findMany` only calls `ensureAuthenticated()` before returning amounts, fees, comments, statuses, and timestamps. See [finance-transactions.handlers.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/server/effect/rpc/handlers/finance/finance-transactions.handlers.ts:14) and [app.routes.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/app/app.routes.ts:41).

- High: event organizer pages have a broken route guard and the backing overview endpoint lacks its own organizer permission check. The route wrappers import guard functions but return the function instead of invoking it with `(route, state)`, so the client guard likely always passes. More importantly, `events.getOrganizeOverview` only requires authentication and returns attendee names/emails for an event. See [events.routes.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/app/events/events.routes.ts:14) and [events-query.handlers.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/server/effect/rpc/handlers/events/events-query.handlers.ts:548).

- High: QR scan lookup is too permissive and incomplete. `events.registrationScanned` only requires authentication and returns attendee/event/registration details for any registration ID in the tenant; the UI’s `checkIn()` handler then does nothing, so the apparent “Confirm Check In” action is not durable. See [events-registration.handlers.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/server/effect/rpc/handlers/events/events-registration.handlers.ts:333) and [handle-registration.component.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/app/scanning/handle-registration/handle-registration.component.ts:40).

- Medium: permission handling is inconsistent and sometimes fail-open in the client. `permissionGuard` returns `true` when route permission metadata is missing, which conflicts with the root “fail closed” guidance. Admin and finance overview links also expose pages before route-level capability checks catch up. See [permission.guard.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/app/core/guards/permission.guard.ts:7) and [admin.routes.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/app/admin/admin.routes.ts:5).

- Medium: Angular/app-guidance drift exists but is not the main risk. There are leftover `standalone: true`, `@HostBinding`, `<mat-icon>`, and `console.*` usages despite Angular 21 and local app rules. These passed lint today, so the repo’s lint config does not currently enforce these documented conventions. Example scan hits include [material-theme.directive.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/app/shared/directives/material-theme.directive.ts:9), [template-list.component.html](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/app/templates/template-list/template-list.component.html:88), and [template-create.component.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/app/templates/template-create/template-create.component.ts:106).

**Validation**

`bunx --bun ng lint --no-fix`: passed.  
`bun run build:app`: passed.  
`bun run test:unit:server`: passed, 21 files / 52 tests.  
`bunx --bun ng test --watch=false`: failed before tests due [tenant-schema.spec.ts](/Users/hedde/code/evorto/src/server/context/tenant-schema.spec.ts:2), specifically importing `@types/custom/tenant` as a runtime module and passing `Tenant` to `Record<string, unknown>`.

No files were edited. Goal marked complete; final elapsed time was 234 seconds, with no token budget set.

# Linear

## Overview

This skill provides a structured workflow for managing issues, projects & team workflows in Linear. It ensures consistent integration with the Linear MCP server, which offers natural-language project management for issues, projects, documentation, and team collaboration.

## Prerequisites
- Linear MCP server must be connected and accessible via OAuth
- Confirm access to the relevant Linear workspace, teams, and projects

## Required Workflow

**Follow these steps in order. Do not skip steps.**

### Step 0: Set up Linear MCP (if not already configured)

If any MCP call fails because Linear MCP is not connected, pause and set it up:

1. Add the Linear MCP:
   - `codex mcp add linear --url https://mcp.linear.app/mcp`
2. Enable remote MCP client:
   - Set `[features] rmcp_client = true` in `config.toml` **or** run `codex --enable rmcp_client`
3. Log in with OAuth:
   - `codex mcp login linear`

After successful login, the user will have to restart codex. You should finish your answer and tell them so when they try again they can continue with Step 1.

**Windows/WSL note:** If you see connection errors on Windows, try configuring the Linear MCP to run via WSL:
```json
{"mcpServers": {"linear": {"command": "wsl", "args": ["npx", "-y", "mcp-remote", "https://mcp.linear.app/sse", "--transport", "sse-only"]}}}
```

### Step 1
Clarify the user's goal and scope (e.g., issue triage, sprint planning, documentation audit, workload balance). Confirm team/project, priority, labels, cycle, and due dates as needed.

### Step 2
Select the appropriate workflow (see Practical Workflows below) and identify the Linear MCP tools you will need. Confirm required identifiers (issue ID, project ID, team key) before calling tools.

### Step 3
Execute Linear MCP tool calls in logical batches:
- Read first (list/get/search) to build context.
- Create or update next (issues, projects, labels, comments) with all required fields.
- For bulk operations, explain the grouping logic before applying changes.

### Step 4
Summarize results, call out remaining gaps or blockers, and propose next actions (additional issues, label changes, assignments, or follow-up comments).

## Available Tools

Issue Management: `list_issues`, `get_issue`, `create_issue`, `update_issue`, `list_my_issues`, `list_issue_statuses`, `list_issue_labels`, `create_issue_label`

Project & Team: `list_projects`, `get_project`, `create_project`, `update_project`, `list_teams`, `get_team`, `list_users`

Documentation & Collaboration: `list_documents`, `get_document`, `search_documentation`, `list_comments`, `create_comment`, `list_cycles`

## Practical Workflows

- Sprint Planning: Review open issues for a target team, pick top items by priority, and create a new cycle (e.g., "Q1 Performance Sprint") with assignments.
- Bug Triage: List critical/high-priority bugs, rank by user impact, and move the top items to "In Progress."
- Documentation Audit: Search documentation (e.g., API auth), then open labeled "documentation" issues for gaps or outdated sections with detailed fixes.
- Team Workload Balance: Group active issues by assignee, flag anyone with high load, and suggest or apply redistributions.
- Release Planning: Create a project (e.g., "v2.0 Release") with milestones (feature freeze, beta, docs, launch) and generate issues with estimates.
- Cross-Project Dependencies: Find all "blocked" issues, identify blockers, and create linked issues if missing.
- Automated Status Updates: Find your issues with stale updates and add status comments based on current state/blockers.
- Smart Labeling: Analyze unlabeled issues, suggest/apply labels, and create missing label categories.
- Sprint Retrospectives: Generate a report for the last completed cycle, note completed vs. pushed work, and open discussion issues for patterns.

## Tips for Maximum Productivity

- Batch operations for related changes; consider smart templates for recurring issue structures.
- Use natural queries when possible ("Show me what John is working on this week").
- Leverage context: reference prior issues in new requests.
- Break large updates into smaller batches to avoid rate limits; cache or reuse filters when listing frequently.

## Troubleshooting

- Authentication: Clear browser cookies, re-run OAuth, verify workspace permissions, ensure API access is enabled.
- Tool Calling Errors: Confirm the model supports multiple tool calls, provide all required fields, and split complex requests.
- Missing Data: Refresh token, verify workspace access, check for archived projects, and confirm correct team selection.
- Performance: Remember Linear API rate limits; batch bulk operations, use specific filters, or cache frequent queries.

Do not modify repository files.\n\nCreate one parent issue titled:\nApp review: close high-risk authorization and consistency findings\n\nParent description:\nA review of the Evorto Angular app and backing app RPC handlers against PRODUCT.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, QUALITY.md, src/app/AGENTS.md, and module guidance found several high-risk gaps. The root text documents are the source of truth: tenant-safe, permission-safe, SSR-safe, type-safe, discoverable workflows with server-side capability checks.\n\nValidation evidence from the review:\n- bunx --bun ng lint --no-fix: passed.\n- bun run build:app: passed.\n- bun run test:unit:server: passed, 21 files / 52 tests.\n- bunx --bun ng test --watch=false: failed before tests because src/server/context/tenant-schema.spec.ts imports @types/custom/tenant as a runtime module and passes Tenant to Record<string, unknown>.\n\nCreate the following subissues under the parent. Use priority High/Urgent for the first four, Medium for the rest if Linear supports priorities. Add concise descriptions with acceptance criteria.\n\nSubissue 1 title: Lock template RPC handlers behind template capabilities\nDescription:\ntemplates.createSimpleTemplate, templates.findOne, templates.groupedByCategory, and templates.updateSimpleTemplate currently only require authentication. This conflicts with template management capabilities defined in shared/permissions/permissions.ts and root product guidance.\nEvidence:\n- src/server/effect/rpc/handlers/templates.handlers.ts:114\n- src/shared/permissions/permissions.ts:32\nAcceptance criteria:\n- Read/list/detail handlers require templates:view or an explicitly documented equivalent.\n- Create requires templates:create.\n- Update requires creator/edit ownership or templates:editAll, using the product's intended template ownership model.\n- Existing template category and tax-rate permission behavior remains coherent.\n- Add or update server tests for denied and allowed cases.\n\nSubissue 2 title: Protect finance transaction history with finance permissions\nDescription:\nfinance.transactions.findMany returns tenant transaction amounts, fees, comments, statuses, and timestamps after only ensureAuthenticated(). The /finance route is also only protected by auth/user-account guards while navigation hides it behind finance:*.\nEvidence:\n- src/server/effect/rpc/handlers/finance/finance-transactions.handlers.ts:14\n- src/app/app.routes.ts:41\n- src/app/core/navigation/navigation.component.html:70\nAcceptance criteria:\n- Server handler requires finance:viewTransactions or a documented stronger finance permission.\n- Finance routes use route-level permission guards matching their server boundaries.\n- Unauthorized users receive a typed forbidden response and cannot load transaction data by direct URL/RPC.\n- Add or update server and route-level tests where practical.\n\nSubissue 3 title: Fix organize route guards and authorize organizer overview on the server\nDescription:\nEvent organize/edit child routes import guard functions but return the function instead of invoking it, so client authorization likely always passes. The backing events.getOrganizeOverview endpoint only requires authentication and returns attendee names/emails for an event.\nEvidence:\n- src/app/events/events.routes.ts:14\n- src/app/events/events.routes.ts:27\n- src/server/effect/rpc/handlers/events/events-query.handlers.ts:548\nAcceptance criteria:\n- Lazy route guards are wired correctly or replaced with direct guard exports that Angular invokes.\n- events.getOrganizeOverview checks can-organize semantics server-side: event organizer registration, events:organizeAll, finance:manageReceipts, or another documented capability.\n- Direct RPC calls by unrelated authenticated users are forbidden.\n- Add or update tests for guard wiring and server authorization.\n\nSubissue 4 title: Restrict QR scan lookup and implement durable check-in\nDescription:\nevents.registrationScanned only requires authentication and returns attendee/event/registration details for any registration id in the tenant. The UI checkIn() method is currently a no-op, so Confirm Check In does not persist check-in state.\nEvidence:\n- src/server/effect/rpc/handlers/events/events-registration.handlers.ts:333\n- src/app/scanning/handle-registration/handle-registration.component.ts:40\nAcceptance criteria:\n- Scan lookup requires check-in/organizer permission for the registration's event.\n- Check-in is implemented as a typed mutation that records checkInTime and updates relevant counts.\n- Existing same-user and non-confirmed registration checks remain enforced server-side.\n- Playwright or server tests cover unauthorized scan, valid scan, and successful check-in.\n\nSubissue 5 title: Make client permission routing fail closed and align admin/finance routes\nDescription:\npermissionGuard currently returns true when route permission metadata is missing. Several admin/finance routes rely on hidden navigation or nested server failures instead of route-level capability checks.\nEvidence:\n- src/app/core/guards/permission.guard.ts:7\n-
